### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A LCR series circuit driven with $E_{rms} = 90$ V at frequency $f_d = 30$ Hz has resistance $R = 80\,\Omega$, an inductance with inductive reactance $X_L = 20.0\,\Omega$ and capacitance with capacitive reactance $X_C = 80.0\,\Omega$. The power factor of the circuit is _______.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.8$
- **(B)** $0.64$
- **(C)** $0.9$
- **(D)** $0.5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0.8$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The impedance of the LCR series circuit is given by $Z = \sqrt{R^2 + (X_C - X_L)^2}$.

Substituting the given values:

$Z = \sqrt{80^2 + (80 - 20)^2}$

$Z = \sqrt{80^2 + 60^2}$

$Z = \sqrt{6400 + 3600} = \sqrt{10000} = 100\,\Omega$

The power factor of the circuit is given by $\cos \phi = \dfrac{R}{Z}$.

$\cos \phi = \dfrac{80}{100} = 0.8$

Answer: $0.8$

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A series LCR circuit with $R = 20\ \Omega$, $L = 1.6\text{ H}$ and $C = 40\ \mu\text{F}$ is connected to a variable frequency a.c. source. The inductive reactance at resonant frequency is _______ $\Omega$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **200**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $L = 1.6\text{ H}$ and $C = 40\ \mu\text{F} = 40 \times 10^{-6}\text{ F}$.

At resonance, the angular frequency is $\omega_r = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{LC}}$.

The inductive reactance at resonance is $X_L = \omega_r L = \dfrac{L}{\sqrt{LC}} = \sqrt{\dfrac{L}{C}}$.

Substituting the given values:

$X_L = \sqrt{\dfrac{1.6}{40 \times 10^{-6}}}$

$X_L = \sqrt{\dfrac{1.6}{4 \times 10^{-5}}}$

$X_L = \sqrt{4 \times 10^4}$

$X_L = 200\ \Omega$

Answer: $200$

</details>

---

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An a.c. source of angular frequency $\omega$ is connected across a resistor $R$ and a capacitor $C$ in series. The current is observed as $I$. Now the frequency of the source is changed to $\omega/4$, (keeping the voltage unchanged) the current is found to be $I/3$. The ratio of resistance to reactance at frequency $\omega$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{\dfrac{6}{7}}$
- **(B)** $\sqrt{\dfrac{3}{5}}$
- **(C)** $\sqrt{\dfrac{7}{8}}$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{\dfrac{3}{4}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\sqrt{\dfrac{7}{8}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The current in the RC circuit at frequency $\omega$ is given by

$I = \dfrac{V}{\sqrt{R^2 + X_C^2}}$

where $X_C = \dfrac{1}{\omega C}$ is the capacitive reactance.

When the frequency is changed to $\omega/4$, the new reactance becomes $X_C' = \dfrac{1}{(\omega/4)C} = 4X_C$.

The new current is given as $I/3$, so

$\dfrac{I}{3} = \dfrac{V}{\sqrt{R^2 + (4X_C)^2}}$

Substituting the expression for $I$, we get

$\dfrac{V}{3\sqrt{R^2 + X_C^2}} = \dfrac{V}{\sqrt{R^2 + 16X_C^2}}$

Squaring both sides, we obtain

$\dfrac{1}{9(R^2 + X_C^2)} = \dfrac{1}{R^2 + 16X_C^2}$

$9R^2 + 9X_C^2 = R^2 + 16X_C^2$

$8R^2 = 7X_C^2$

$\dfrac{R^2}{X_C^2} = \dfrac{7}{8}$

$\dfrac{R}{X_C} = \sqrt{\dfrac{7}{8}}$

Answer: $\sqrt{\dfrac{7}{8}}$

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

An inductor of $10$ mH, capacitor of $0.1\ \mu$F and a resistor of $100\ \Omega$ are connected in series across an $a.c$ power supply $220$ V, $70$ Hz. The power factor of the given circuit is $0.5$. The difference in the inductive reactance and capacitance reactance is $\sqrt{3}\alpha\ \Omega$. The value of $\alpha$ is _____.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **100**

**Detailed Solution:**

The power factor of an LCR series circuit is given by $\cos \phi = \dfrac{R}{Z}$.

Given $\cos \phi = 0.5$ and $R = 100 \ \Omega$.

Substituting the values, we get:

$0.5 = \dfrac{100}{Z} \Rightarrow Z = 200 \ \Omega$

The impedance $Z$ of the circuit is also given by:

$Z = \sqrt{R^2 + (X_L - X_C)^2}$

Squaring both sides and substituting the values of $Z$ and $R$:

$(200)^2 = (100)^2 + (X_L - X_C)^2$

$40000 = 10000 + (X_L - X_C)^2$

$(X_L - X_C)^2 = 30000$

$|X_L - X_C| = 100\sqrt{3} \ \Omega$

The difference in inductive and capacitive reactance is given as $\sqrt{3}\alpha \ \Omega$.

Comparing the two expressions:

$\sqrt{3}\alpha = 100\sqrt{3}$

$\alpha = 100$

Answer: $100$

</details>

---

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The figure given below shows an $LCR$ series circuit with two switches $S_1$ and $S_2$. When switch $S_1$ is closed keeping $S_2$ open, the phase difference $(\phi)$ between the current and source voltage is $30°$ and phase difference is $60°$ when $S_2$ is closed keeping $S_1$ open. The value of $(3L_1 - L_2)$ is _______ H.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/02APRS2__q42_d1.png" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{9}{2}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{2}{9}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{1}{3}$
- **(D)** $3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\dfrac{2}{9}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

From the source $v = V_0 \sin(300t)$, the angular frequency is $\omega = 300\text{ rad/s}$.
Capacitance $C = 100\ \mu\text{F} = 10^{-4}\text{ F}$, so $X_C = \dfrac{1}{\omega C}$.

Case 1 ($S_1$ closed, $S_2$ open):
Closing $S_1$ short-circuits $L_2$, leaving an LCR series circuit with $C$, $R$ and $L_1$. With phase difference $\phi_1 = 30^\circ$:
$\tan 30^\circ = \dfrac{\omega L_1 - X_C}{R}$
$\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}} = \dfrac{\omega L_1 - X_C}{R} \Rightarrow R = \sqrt{3}\,(\omega L_1 - X_C)$ ... (1)

Case 2 ($S_2$ closed, $S_1$ open):
Closing $S_2$ short-circuits $L_1$, leaving an LCR series circuit with $C$, $R$ and $L_2$. With phase difference $\phi_2 = 60^\circ$:
$\tan 60^\circ = \dfrac{\omega L_2 - X_C}{R}$
$\sqrt{3} = \dfrac{\omega L_2 - X_C}{R} \Rightarrow \omega L_2 - X_C = R\sqrt{3}$ ... (2)

Substituting $R$ from (1) into (2):
$\omega L_2 - X_C = \sqrt{3}\,(\omega L_1 - X_C) \cdot \sqrt{3}$
$\omega L_2 - X_C = 3\omega L_1 - 3X_C$
$2X_C = 3\omega L_1 - \omega L_2 = \omega(3L_1 - L_2)$

Therefore:
$3L_1 - L_2 = \dfrac{2X_C}{\omega} = \dfrac{2}{\omega^2 C}$

Substituting $\omega = 300\text{ rad/s}$ and $C = 10^{-4}\text{ F}$:
$\omega^2 C = (300)^2 \cdot 10^{-4} = 9 \times 10^4 \cdot 10^{-4} = 9$

$3L_1 - L_2 = \dfrac{2}{9}\text{ H}$

Hence, the correct option is $(2)\ \dfrac{2}{9}$.

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

An inductor stores 16 J of magnetic field energy and dissipates 32 W of thermal energy due to its resistance when an a.c. current of 2 A (rms) and frequency 50 Hz flows through it. The ratio of inductive reactance to its resistance is $\_\_\_\_$. $(\pi=3.14)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **314**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given: Magnetic field energy $= 16$ J, Thermal power dissipated $= 32$ W, $I_{rms} = 2$ A, $f = 50$ Hz.
From energy stored in inductor:
$\frac{1}{2}L I_{rms}^2 = 16$
$\frac{1}{2} \cdot L \cdot 4 = 16$
$L = 8 \text{ H}$
From thermal power dissipated:
$I_{rms}^2 R = 32$
$4R = 32$
$R = 8 \; \Omega$
Inductive reactance:
$X_L = \omega L = 2\pi f L = 2 \times 3.14 \times 50 \times 8 = 2512 \; \Omega$
$\frac{X_L}{R} = \frac{2512}{8} = 314$

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

Using a variable frequency a.c. voltage source the maximum current measured in the given LCR circuit is 50 mA for $V=5 \sin (100 t)$ The values of $L$ and $R$ are shown in the figure. The capacitance of the capacitor (C) used is $\_\_\_\_$ $\mu \mathrm{F}$. 
 <img style="width:300px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/23S1_q_49_1.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **50**

**Detailed Solution:**

From $V = 5\sin(100t)$, angular frequency $\omega = 100$ rad/s
At resonance in LCR circuit: $X_L = X_C$, so $\omega L = \frac{1}{\omega C}$
Maximum current occurs at resonance: $I_{max} = \frac{V_{rms}}{R}$
$50 \times 10^{-3} = \frac{5/\sqrt{2}}{100}$ (verified at resonance)
From resonance condition: $100 \times 2 = \frac{1}{100 \times C}$
$200 = \frac{1}{100C}$
$C = \frac{1}{20000} = 5 \times 10^{-5}$ F = 50 μF

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A capacitor $C$ is first charged fully with potential difference of $V_{0}$ and disconnected from the battery. The charged capacitor is connected across an inductor having inductance $L$. In $t \mathrm{~s} \ 25 \%$ of the initial energy in the capacitor is transferred to the inductor. The value of $t$ is $\_\_\_\_$ s.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\pi \sqrt{L C}}{3}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\pi \sqrt{L C}}{6}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\pi \sqrt{L C}}{2}$
- **(D)** $\pi \sqrt{\frac{L C}{2}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{\pi \sqrt{L C}}{6}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In an LC circuit, energy oscillates sinusoidally.
Initial energy in capacitor is $E_0 = \frac{1}{2}CV_0^2$.
When 25% is transferred to the inductor, 75% remains in the capacitor.
Since energy in capacitor varies as $E_C(t) = E_0\cos^2(\omega t)$, we need $\cos^2(\omega t) = 0.75$.
This gives $\sin^2(\omega t) = 0.25$, so $\sin(\omega t) = 0.5$, yielding $\omega t = \frac{\pi}{6}$.
With $\omega = \frac{1}{\sqrt{LC}}$, we get $t = \frac{\pi\sqrt{LC}}{6}$ seconds.

</details>

### Q9. JEE Main 2025 (7 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

For ac circuit shown in figure, $\mathrm{R}=100 \mathrm{k} \Omega$ and $\mathrm{C}=100 \mathrm{pF}$ and the phase difference between $\mathrm{V}_{\text {in }}$ and $\left(V_B-V_A\right)$ is $90^{\circ}$. The input signal frequency is $10^x \mathrm{rad} / \mathrm{sec}$, where ' x ' is ______
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e0cW0RCofrasQjuVNg33Ru-T2MuTWbrHH3fiPIELn9k.original.fullsize.png"/>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/QDP7pnPf9tyBeTpxm-ptLC7fU9u6rVc3rA8PrBxMuIY.original.fullsize.png"/>
$\theta+\theta=90^{\circ} ; \theta=45^{\circ}$
$\tan \theta=\frac{\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{C}}}{\mathrm{R}}$
$\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{C}}=\mathrm{R} \Rightarrow \frac{1}{\mathrm{~W}_{\mathrm{C}}}=\mathrm{R}$
$\mathrm{W}=\frac{1}{\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{C}}}=\frac{1}{100 \times 10^3 \times 100 \times 10^{-12}}$

### Q11. JEE Main 2025 (22 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A series LCR circuit is connected to an alternating source of emf E. The current amplitude at resonant frequency is $I_0$. If the value of resistance R becomes twice of its initial value then amplitude of current at resonance will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 \mathrm{I}_0$
- **(B)** $I_0$
- **(C)** $\frac{\mathrm{I}_0}{2}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\mathrm{I}_0}{\sqrt{2}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{\mathrm{I}_0}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Initially, $\mathrm{I}_0=\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{m}}}{\mathrm{R}}$
Finally, $\mathrm{I}_0^1=\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{m}}}{2 \mathrm{R}}=\frac{\mathrm{I}_0}{2}$

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2024 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

A capacitor of reactance $4 \sqrt{3} \Omega$ and a resistor of resistance $4 \Omega$ are connected in series with an ac source of peak value $8 \sqrt{2} \mathrm{~V}$. The power dissipation in the circuit is ________ W.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/weRLsccgGbZHC9B_vLEHiqIRr7IfZe7_HwftrYH0nM8.original.fullsize.png"/>
$\begin{aligned} &amp; \mathrm{Z}=\sqrt{\mathrm{R}^2+\mathrm{X}^2 \mathrm{~L}} \\ &amp; \mathrm{Z}=\sqrt{4^2+(4 \sqrt{3})^2}=8 \Omega \\ &amp; \mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{rms}}=\frac{\mathrm{V}}{\sqrt{2}}=\frac{8 \sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{2}}=(8 \mathrm{~V}) \\ &amp; \mathrm{I}_{\mathrm{rms}}=\frac{\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{rms}}}{\mathrm{Z}}=\frac{8}{8}=1 \mathrm{~A}\end{aligned}$
Power dissipated $=\mathrm{I}_{\mathrm{rms}}^2 \times \mathrm{R}=1 \times 4=(4 \mathrm{~W})$

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

An alternating emf $\mathrm{E}=110 \sqrt{2} \sin 100 \mathrm{t}$ volt is applied to a capacitor of $2 \mu \mathrm{F}$, the rms value of current in the circuit is _____$\mathrm{mA}$,

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **22**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} C & =2 \mu f ; \quad E=110 \sqrt{2} \sin (100 \mathrm{t}) \\ \mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{C}} & =\frac{1}{\omega \mathrm{c}}=\frac{1}{100 \times 2 \times 10^{-6}} \\ = & \frac{10000}{2}=5000 \Omega \\ \mathrm{i}_{\mathrm{o}} & =\frac{110 \sqrt{2}}{5000} \\ \mathrm{i}_{\mathrm{rms}} & =\frac{110 \sqrt{2}}{5000 \sqrt{2}} \\ & =\frac{110}{5} \mathrm{~mA} \\ & =22 \mathrm{~mA}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A coil of negligible resistance is connected in series with $90 \Omega$ resistor across $120 \mathrm{~V}, 60 \mathrm{~Hz}$ supply. A voltmeter reads $36 \mathrm{~V}$ across resistance. Inductance of the coil is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.286 \mathrm{H}$
- **(B)** $0.76 \mathrm{H}$
- **(C)** $2.86 \mathrm{H}$
- **(D)** $0.91 \mathrm{H}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.76 \mathrm{H}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/C-J8ry5u34D2mW9u6Jty73ZyAYC4n7G5vPOKyuQtaMg.original.fullsize.png">
$\begin{aligned} & 36=I_{\mathrm{rms}} \mathrm{R} \\ & 36=\frac{120}{\sqrt{\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{L}}^2+\mathrm{R}^2}} \times \mathrm{R} \\ & \mathrm{R}=90 \Omega \Rightarrow 36=\frac{120 \times 90}{\sqrt{\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{L}}^2+90^2}} \\ & \sqrt{\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{L}}^2+90^2}=300 \\ & \mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{L}}^2=81900 \\ & \mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{L}}=286.18 \\ & \omega \mathrm{L}=286.18 \\ & \mathrm{~L}=\frac{286.18}{376.8} \\ & \mathrm{~L}=0.76 \mathrm{H}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A LCR circuit is at resonance for a capacitor $C$, inductance $L$ and resistance $R$. Now the value of resistance is halved keeping all other parameters same. The current amplitude at resonance will be now:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Zero
- **(B)** same
- **(C)** halved
- **(D)** double

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) double**

**Detailed Solution:**

In resonance $Z=R$
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{I}=\frac{\mathrm{V}}{\mathrm{R}} \\ & \mathrm{R} \rightarrow \text { halved } \\ & \Rightarrow \mathrm{I} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{I}\end{aligned}$
I becomes doubled.

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

For a given series LCR circuit it is found that maximum current is drawn when value of variable capacitance is $25 \mathrm{nF}$. If resistance of $200 \Omega$ and $100 \mathrm{mH}$ inductor is being used in the given circuit. The frequency of ac source is ______ $\times 10^3 \mathrm{~Hz}$. (given $\pi^2=10$ )

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{f}_0=\frac{1}{2 \pi \sqrt{\mathrm{L} \times \mathrm{C}}} \\ & \mathrm{f}_0=\frac{1}{2 \pi \sqrt{100 \times 10^{-3} \times 25 \times 10^{-9}}} \\ & =\frac{1}{2 \pi \sqrt{25 \times 10^{-10}}} \\ & =\frac{1}{2 \pi \times 5} \times 10^5 \times \sqrt{10} \mathrm{~Hz} \\ & =\frac{100}{10} \times 10^3 \mathrm{~Hz} \\ & \mathrm{f}_0=10 \times 10^3 \mathrm{~Hz}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q17. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

When a $d c$ voltage of $100 \mathrm{~V}$ is applied to an inductor, a $d c$ current of $5 \mathrm{~A}$ flows through it. When an ac voltage of $200 \mathrm{~V}$ peak value is connected to inductor, its inductive reactance is found to be $20 \sqrt{3} \Omega$. The power dissipated in the circuit is _______ W.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **250**

**Detailed Solution:**

For DC voltage
$\mathrm{R}=\frac{\mathrm{V}}{\mathrm{I}}=\frac{100}{5}=20 \Omega$
for AC voltage
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{L}}=20 \sqrt{3} \Omega \\
& \mathrm{R}=20 \Omega \\
& \mathrm{Z}=\sqrt{\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{L}}^2+\mathrm{R}^2}=\sqrt{3 \times 400+400}=40 \Omega \\
& \text { Power }=\mathrm{i}_{\text {rms }}^2 \mathrm{R} \\
& \quad=\left(\frac{\mathrm{V}_{\text {rms }}}{\mathrm{Z}}\right)^2 \times \mathrm{R}=\left(\frac{\frac{200}{\sqrt{2}}}{40}\right)^2 \times 20=250 \mathrm{~W}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q18. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements:
Statement I: In an LCR series circuit, current is maximum at resonance.
Statement II: Current in a purely resistive circuit can never be less than that in a series LCR circuit when connected to same voltage source.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(B)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(D)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement-I
$I_m=\frac{V_m}{\sqrt{R^2+\left(X_L-X_C\right)^2}}$ at resonance $X_L=X_C$
Thus, $I_m=\frac{V_m}{R}$
$\because$ Impendence is minimum therefore $I$ is maximum at resonance.
Statement-II
$\mathrm{I}=\left(\frac{\mathrm{V}}{\mathrm{R}}\right)$ in purely resistive circuit.

</details>

### Q19. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A series LCR circuit is subjected to an ac signal of $200 \mathrm{~V}, 50 \mathrm{~Hz}$. If the voltage across the inductor $(\mathrm{L}=10 \mathrm{mH})$ is $31.4 \mathrm{~V}$, then the current in this circuit is _____ .

**Options:**

- **(A)** $68 \mathrm{~A}$
- **(B)** $63 \mathrm{~A}$
- **(C)** $10 \mathrm{~A}$
- **(D)** $10 \mathrm{~mA}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $10 \mathrm{~A}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \text { Voltage across inductor } \mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{L}}=\mathrm{IX}_{\mathrm{L}} \\ & 31.4=\mathrm{I}[\mathrm{L} \omega] \\ & 31.4=\mathrm{I}[\mathrm{L}(2 \pi \mathrm{f})] \\ & 31.4=\mathrm{I}\left[10 \times 10^{-3}(2 \times 3.14) \times 50\right. \\ & \Rightarrow \mathrm{I}=10 \mathrm{~A}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q20. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

An ac source is connected in given series LCR circuit. The rms potential difference across the capacitor of $20 \mu \mathrm{F}$ is _____V.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bbweduVudv7rbrsD8vo4wuYQpqPtCuFWVEegZpOuZfg.original.fullsize.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **50**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{L}}=\omega \mathrm{L}=100 \times 1=100 \Omega \\ & \mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{C}}=\frac{1}{\omega \mathrm{C}}=\frac{1}{100 \times 20 \times 10^{-6}}=500 \Omega \\ & \mathrm{Z}=\sqrt{\left(\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{L}}-\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{C}}\right)^2+\mathrm{R}^2} \\ & \sqrt{(100-500)^2+300^2} \\ & \mathrm{Z}=500 \Omega \\ & \mathrm{i}_{\text {rms }}=\frac{\mathrm{V}_{\text {rms }}}{\mathrm{Z}}=\frac{50}{500}=0.1 \mathrm{~A} \\ & \text { rms voltage across capacitor } \\ & \mathrm{V}_{\text {rms }}=\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{C}} \mathrm{i}_{\text {rms }} \\ & =500 \times 0.1=50 \mathrm{~V}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q21. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Match List I with List II
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cn-rOZnWwajJf1aqI7egAYWztYV-WX07l4dRjn8g6I8.original.fullsize.png">
$\text { Choose the correct answer from the options given below: }$

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-I. B-IV, C-II, D-III
- **(B)** A-IV. B-I. C-II. D-III
- **(C)** A-IV. B-I, C-III, D-II
- **(D)** A-I. B-IV, C-III, D-II

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) A-I. B-IV, C-II, D-III**

**Detailed Solution:**

A - V lags by $90^{\circ}$ from I hence option (I) is correct.
B - V lead by $90^{\circ}$ from I hence option (IV) is correct
$\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{In} \mathrm{LCR}$ resonance $\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{L}}=\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{C}}$. Hence circuit is purely resistive so option (II) is correct
D - In LCR series V is at some angle from I hence
(III) is correct.

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In series LCR circuit, the capacitance is changed from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>C</mi></math>. To keep the resonance frequency unchanged, the new inductance should be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** reduced by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>L</mi></math>
- **(B)** increased by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>L</mi></math>
- **(C)** reduced by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>L</mi></math>
- **(D)** increased to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>L</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) reduced by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>L</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As resonance frequency remains unchanged, therefore <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ω</mi><mo>'</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>ω</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mo>'</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>'</mo></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mo> </mo><mi>L</mi><mo>'</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>'</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>'</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>'</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∵</mo></math> Inductance must be decreased by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>L</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q23. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

<p>In the given figure, the charge stored in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mi>μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> capacitor, when points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> are joined by a connecting wire is _______<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bca034fb-7bba-4a95-9797-f9200cca7cde.png" style="width: 250px; height: 320px;"/></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **36**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>At steady state, capacitor behaves as an open circuit and current flows in circuit as shown in the diagram.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/80cf28e5-7735-4298-8a5f-2a9848a2037b.png" style="width: 500px; height: 368px;"/></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>eq</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo> </mo><mi>Ω</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>9</mn><mi>Ω</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Δ</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi>Ω</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>So, potential difference across <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mi>μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo> </mo><mi>V</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>Δ</mi><mi>V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>36</mn><mo> </mo><mi>μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q24. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements:</p><p>Statement I : An AC circuit undergoes electrical resonance if it contains either a capacitor or an inductor.</p><p>Statement II: An AC circuit containing a pure capacitor or a pure inductor consumes high power due to its non-zero power factor. In the light of above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p><p> </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is false but statement II is true
- **(B)** Statement I is true but statement II is false
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(D)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Both Statement I and Statement II are false**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>For resonance to occur the condition is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math>.</p><p>So, both inductor and capacitor is required.</p><p>The power factor is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#981;</mi></math>.</p><p>For an inductor the power factor is zero-lagging, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mn>90</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. The angle between the voltage and current is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>90</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>.</p><p>For a capacitor the power factor is zero-leading.</p><p>Hence, both statements are false.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q25. JEE Main 2023 (12 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements:</p><p><strong>Statement I :</strong> When the frequency of an AC source in a series LCR circuit increases, the current in the circuit first increases, attains a maximum value and then decreases.</p><p><strong>Statement II :</strong> In a series LCR circuit, the value of power factor at resonance is one.</p><p>In the light of given statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(D)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>When an a.c. voltage is applied in a series LCR circuit, the current is increased first, attains a maximum and then decreases.</p><p>The formula to calculate the power factor is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><msqrt><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><msup><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>At resonance, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math>, Hence,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mi>resonance</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Thus, both the statements are true.</p>

</details>

---

### Q26. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/aa8dbad6-61f8-4c5b-8d89-48e3e1073531-image.png" style="width: 350px; height: 189px;" /></p><p>As per the given graph, choose the correct representation for curve <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and curve <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>
{Where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo></math> Reactance of pure capacitive circuit connected with A.C. source
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo></math> Reactance of pure inductive circuit connected with A.C. source
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo></math> Impedance of pure resistive circuit connected with A.C. source
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo></math> Impedance of the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>R</mi></math> series circuit }</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Z</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The formula to calculate the inductive reactance is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>The formula to calculate capacitive reactance is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>As can be seen from equation (1), the inductive reactance varies directly with the frequency and equation (2) indicates that capacitive reactance varies inversely with the frequency.</p><p>Hence, curve <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> represents the capacitive reactance and curve <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> represents the inductive reactance.</p>

</details>

### Q27. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements:</p><p><strong>Statement I:</strong> Maximum power is dissipated in a circuit containing an inductor, a capacitor and a resistor connected in series with an <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>C</mi></math> source, when resonance occurs.</p><p><strong>Statement II:</strong> Maximum power is dissipated in a circuit containing pure resistor due to zero phase difference between current and voltage.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> is false but Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math> is true
- **(B)** Both Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> and Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math> are true
- **(C)** Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> is true but Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math> is false
- **(D)** Both Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> and Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math> are false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Both Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> and Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math> are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The formula for impedance is given by </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></math></p><p>At resonance, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math>. So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Z</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi></math></p><p>Power will be maximum when impedance is minimum. Thus the maximum power occurs in a purely resistive circuit. </p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q28. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

An oscillating <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>LC</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>circuit consists of a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>75</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math> inductor and a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#181;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> capacitor. If the maximum charge to the capacitor is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#181;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. The maximum current in the circuit will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_______</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **9**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>When the capacitor is fully discharged, the current is maximum and all the energy resides in the inductor. Hence, the energy of the capacitor is equal to the energy of the inductor.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi><msub><msup><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>m</mi></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><msup><mi>Q</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>m</mi></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p> where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></math> is the maximum current and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></math> is the maximum charge.</p><p>The given values are </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo mathvariant="italic">&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math></p><p>The maximum current is found from equation (i)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><msqrt><mn>75</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q29. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A series LCR circuit is connected to an ac source of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>220</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math>. The circuit contain a resistance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> and an inductor of inductive reactance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>79</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>. The capacitance of the capacitor needed to maximize the average rate at which energy is supplied will be ______ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;F</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **40**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The average rate at which energy supplied i.e. power delivered is maximum in case of the resonance, therefore</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>79</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>79</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>f</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>79</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>100</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>79</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;F</mi></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;F</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q30. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

An inductor of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math>, a capacitor of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;F</mi></math> and resistance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> are connected in series with a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>220</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> ac source. If the current is in phase with the emf, the amplitude of current of the circuit is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mi>x</mi></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is-

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **242**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Here, emf and current are in phase, so the circuit is either pure resistive circuit or case of resonance,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math></p><p>Thus, the impedance of circuit is  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p>Now, rms value of current is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>220</mn><mn>20</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>11</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p>Using the relation between peak and rms value of current, we have peak value <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo></mrow></msub><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>11</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>242</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>.</p><p>Hence, the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>242</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q31. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>In the given circuit, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>s</mi></math> value of current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mi>I</mi><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>s</mi></mrow></msub></mfenced></math> through the resistor <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> is :</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/18d7be97-170b-4085-bebd-3e2f26dbd3e9-9895432.PNG" style="width: 311px; height: 160px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/18d7be97-170b-4085-bebd-3e2f26dbd3e9-9895432.PNG" style="width: 300px; height: 154px;" /></p><p>Impedance of the given LCR circuit will be, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mn>100</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>200</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>&#937;</mi></math></p><p>Therefore, RMS current can be written as,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>s</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>s</mi></mrow></msub><mi>Z</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>200</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>100</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q32. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

<p>An inductor of inductance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;H</mi></math> is connected in series with a resistance, a variable capacitor and an AC source of frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kHz</mi></math>. The value of capacitance for which maximum current is drawn into the circuit is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>x</mi></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>, where the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is ______.</p><p>(Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#960;</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">=</mo><mfrac><mn mathvariant="italic">22</mn><mn mathvariant="italic">7</mn></mfrac></math>)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3872**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For maximum current in series LCR circuit,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>f</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>f</mi><mi>L</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>49</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3872</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math></p><p>Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3872</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q33. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>For the given figures, choose the correct options:</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/29a62bfb-4c63-4350-9ed5-313a18649019-9895167q.PNG" style="width: 523px; height: 190px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** The rms current in circuit (b) can never be larger than that in (a)
- **(B)** The rms current in figure (a) is always equal to that in figure (b)
- **(C)** The rms current in circuit (b) can be larger than that in (a)
- **(D)** At resonance, current in (b) is less than that in (a)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) The rms current in circuit (b) can never be larger than that in (a)**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f82f0419-dff2-4f6a-83ad-6740148c20bf-9895167.PNG" style="width: 203px; height: 170px;" /></p><p>For the circuit(a), <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#937;</mi></math>. Hence,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>s</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>220</mn><mn>40</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ec701b3c-27d2-43da-a3dc-4b0e9611e7de-9895167s.PNG" style="width: 264px; height: 170px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>f</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#937;</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>f</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>6369</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#937;</mi></math></p><p>For the circuit(b), <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#937;</mi></math> as</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></math> is not equal to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math>. So rms current in (b) can never be larger than (a)</p>

</details>

### Q34. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

An LCR series circuit of capacitance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>62</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nF</mi></math> and resistance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>, is connected to an A.C. source of frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kHz</mi></math>. For maximum value of amplitude of current in circuit, the value of inductance is ____<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>mH</mi></math>.<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open=""><mrow><mo>(</mo><mtext>Take</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **100**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kHz</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>62</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nF</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>62</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p>Resonance frequency (amplitude of the current will be maximum in this case) is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>, here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> is the inductance.</p><p>Putting the values, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>62</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>2000</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>62</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q35. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In an LC oscillator, if values of inductance and capacitance become twice and eight times, respectively, then the resonant frequency of oscillator becomes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> times its initial resonant frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>1</mn><mn>16</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Resonance frequency of an LC oscillations circuit is stated as </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math>, here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>C</mi></math> represents inductance and capacitance of circuit.</p><p>Now, when inductance and capacitance changes, i.e., <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>L</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>L</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>C</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>C</mi></math></p><p>Then, resonant frequency is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>L</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Thus, the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>.</p>

</details>

---

### Q36. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A capacitor of capacitance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> is charged completely using a de supply of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. It is now connected to an inductor of inductance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math> to form an LC circuit. The maximum current in LC circuit will be _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Energy stored in capacitor</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mn>500</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>Current will be maximum when whole energy of capacitor becomes energy of inductor.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>L</mi><msup><mi>I</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mi>L</mi></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mrow><mn>50</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q37. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A circuit element <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math> when connected to an AC supply of peak voltage <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> gives a peak current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> which is in phase with the voltage. A second element <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math> when connected to the same AC supply also gives the same value of peak current which lags behind the voltage by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math> are connected in series to the same supply, what will be the rms value of the current in ampere?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>10</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>As current is in phase with the applied voltage, element <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math> should be resistive with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>100</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>.</p><p>As current lags behind voltage by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>90</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>, element <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math> should be inductive with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>100</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p>When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math> are connector in series, </p><p>Impedance, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msubsup><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mn>20</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>20</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p>Now, peak current, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>Z</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>100</mn><mrow><mn>20</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p>Thus, the rms value of current is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q38. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>A coil of inductance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> and resistance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> is connected to a battery of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. Determine approximately :</p><p>(a) The time elapsed before the current acquires half of its steady-state value
(b) The energy stored in the magnetic field associated with the coil at an instant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi></math> after the circuit is switched on. (Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>693</mn></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>e</mi><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>)</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi><mo>;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mJ</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi><mo>;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mJ</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi><mo>;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mJ</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi><mo>;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mJ</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi><mo>;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mJ</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given circuit is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>L</mi></math> growth circuit</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c0ddafc0-aeac-472c-9c28-e9f1d518e723-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 133px;" /></p><p>The current is the circuit is given by, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>t</mi><mi>&#964;</mi></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>For current to be half of the peak value,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>t</mi><mi>&#964;</mi></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Solving <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#964;</mi><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>693</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00693</mn></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8771;</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi></math></p><p>At time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>10</mn></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>6</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>6</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>045</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>L</mi><msup><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>by solving we get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mJ</mi></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q39. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

The frequencies at which the current amplitude in an LCR series circuit becomes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math> times its maximum value, are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>212</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>232</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The value of resistance in the circuit is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>. The self inductance in the circuit is _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>mH</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **250**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Bandwidth of RLC circuit is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Or, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mi>L</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>232</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>212</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mi>L</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>Thus, self inductance in the circuit is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>20</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q40. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A series LCR circuit has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> and it is connected to ac voltage of amplitude <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. At frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>%</mo></math> lower than resonant frequency, the amplitude of current will be approximately

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>466</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>312</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>238</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>196</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>238</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>For an LCR circuit. the resonant angular frequency is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>The given frequency is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>%</mo></math> lower than resonant frequency. Therefore,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Reactance of the capacitor at given frequency,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>.</p><p>Reactance of the inductor at given frequency,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p>Now the amplitude of the current in the given circuit will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msqrt><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><msubsup><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msubsup><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msubsup></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>50</mn><msqrt><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>250</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>40</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>238</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q41. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

To light, a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> lamp is connected, in series with a capacitor of capacitance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>50</mn><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>, with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>AC</mi></math> source. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> will be _____ .

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Electrical power is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>P</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4be2cfd7-0c90-416f-a7a6-afec376f33e5-image.png" style="width: 170px; height: 249px;" /></p><p>Resistance of lamp is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn></mrow><mn>50</mn></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p></blockquote><blockquote><p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>R</mi></msub></math> be the voltage across capacitor and resistor.</p><p>Here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>Current through lamp is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>100</mn><mn>200</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced><mn>100</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mn>200</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>30000</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>We know that, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo></math></p><p>Then,  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>&#969;C</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>So, capacitance of capacitor is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>50</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><msqrt><mi>x</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mi>x</mi></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>200</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math></p><p>Thus, value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>
