### Q42. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

<p>The effective current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi></math> in the given circuit at very high frequencies will be _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9f9ba65c-1fac-4c78-84ae-1540a01f43ef-image.png" style="width: 450px; height: 179px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **44**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>At very high frequencies, inductor acts as an open circuit and capacitor as short circuit.</p><p>So, capacitive reactance, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&omega;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&asymp;</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Also, inductive reactance, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&omega;</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>&asymp;</mo><mo>&infin;</mo></math></p><p>Thus, equivalent circuit can be redrawn as</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/081e3cb9-74b3-4607-9748-d73c3d7ea6da-image.png" style="width: 180px; height: 147px;" /></p><p>Here, both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&Omega;</mi></math> resistances are in parallel and together they are in series with<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&Omega;</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&Omega;</mi></math> resistances.</p><p>Equivalent impedance is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&times;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&Omega;</mi></math></p><p>Effective current is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>220</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&Omega;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>44</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q43. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

When you walk through a metal detector carrying a metal object in your pocket, it raises an alarm. This phenomenon works on

**Options:**

- **(A)** Electromagnetic induction
- **(B)** Resonance in ac circuits
- **(C)** Mutual induction in ac circuits
- **(D)** interference of electromagnetic waves

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Resonance in ac circuits**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Metal detector works on the principle of transmitting an electromagnetic signal and analyses a return signal from the target. So it works on the principle of resonance in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AC</mi></math> circuit.</p><p>When the LC circuit detects any resonating frequency from any metal which is near to it, electric field will be created which will lead to induces current in the coil and changes in the signal flow through the coil.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q44. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The RMS value of conduction current in a parallel plate capacitor is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>. The capacity of this capacitor, if it is connected to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>230</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> AC supply with an angular frequency of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>pF</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mi>pF</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mi>pF</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mi>pF</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mi>pF</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;A</mi><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>230</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Current in capacitor <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></mfrac></math>, where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math> is capacitive reactance given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Now, current, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, capacitance is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mfrac><msub><mi>I</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>230</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>600</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>pF</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q45. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

To increase the resonant frequency in series LCR circuit,

**Options:**

- **(A)** Source frequency should be increased
- **(B)** Another resistance should be added in series with the first resistance.
- **(C)** Another capacitor should be added in series with the first capacitor
- **(D)** The source frequency should be decreased

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Another capacitor should be added in series with the first capacitor**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The resonant frequency of an LCR circuit is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>. It does not depend on the source frequency as well as the resistance of the circuit.</p><p>Therefore, to increase the resonating frequency product of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> should decrease.</p><p>By joining another capacitor in series, the equivalent capacitor will decrease.</p><p>Hence, option C is correct option.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q46. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>For a series <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>R</mi></math> circuit, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi></math> vs <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi></math> curve is shown </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f01f324e-9e8b-4c63-9754-3bd1bb881fc8-image.png" style="width: 210px; height: 178px;" /></p><p>(a) To the left of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></math>, the circuit is mainly capacitive.
(b) To the left of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></math>, the circuit is mainly inductive.
(c) At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></math>, impedance of the circuit is equal to the resistance of the circuit.
(d) At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></math>, impedance of the circuit is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math>.</p><p>Choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** (a) and (d) only
- **(B)** (b) and (d) only
- **(C)** (a) and (c) only
- **(D)** (b) and (c) only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) (a) and (c) only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The impedance of an AC circuit is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></math>.</p><p>For current to be maximum <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi></math> should be minimum. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi></math> will be minimum when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. Therefore, the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi></math>. Hence, statement C is correct and statement D is wrong.</p><p>To the left of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></math> i.e. when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></math>. Therefore, the circuit is mainly capacitive. Hence, statement A is correct and statement B is wrong.</p>

</details>

### Q47. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

<p>In the given circuit, the magnitude of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math> are twice that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>R</mi></msub></math>. Given that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math>, the inductance of the coil is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>K</mi><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi></math> is _____ .</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c0db48be-69b5-4054-a042-a1a42a78fddf-image.png" style="width: 250px; height: 210px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **0**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Since the voltage across the inductor and capacitor is same, the circuit is in resonance. Therefore, the current in the circuit will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>V</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>220</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>44</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>.</p><p>The voltage across the inductor is double that across the resistor. Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>R</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>440</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>.</p><p>We know that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>i</mi><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>f</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>440</mn><mrow><mn>44</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>100</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mfrac><mi>mH</mi></math></p><p>Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q49. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>110</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>AC</mi></math> source is connected in the circuit (as shown in figure). The current through the resistance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>55</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>, at resonance in the circuit, will be _____<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3e475d87-e3a6-4e4b-bcb9-539ed44954fe-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 171px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **0**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let current in the inductance is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></math>, through the capacitor is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math> and through resistance is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>R</mi></msub></math> at any instance as shown in the figure.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/556ecce3-c9ac-4b4c-b3f9-b01aa0fe7853-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 166px;" /></p><p>According to the Kirchoff&#39;s junction law, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>R</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math>.</p><p>At resonance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></math> hence <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math>. Also, current through the capacitor and inductor have phase difference of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi></math>. Therefore, they will form destructive interference and hence the current through the resistance will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q50. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

<p>In a series LCR circuit, the inductance, capacitance and resistance are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> respectively. They are connected to an <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AC</mi></math> source of voltage <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>220</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> and frequency of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math>. The approximate value of current in the circuit will be _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math>.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/837d7654-afbd-4673-8886-887dd9e77e78-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 145px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **22**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Angular frequency, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Here, inductive reactance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>100</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p>And capacitive reactance<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>100</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>100</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p>Now, impedance of the series circuit is  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p>Therefore,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mi>RMS</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>RMS</mi></msub><mi>Z</mi></mfrac></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>220</mn><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>22</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q51. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If wattless current flows in the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AC</mi></math> circuit, then the circuit is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Purely Resistive circuit
- **(B)** Purely Inductive circuit
- **(C)** LCR series circuit
- **(D)** RC series circuit only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Purely Inductive circuit**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The current in an AC circuit containing only either a capacitor or an inductor is said to be wattless as the average power is zero. As wattless current has phase difference of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> with voltage, therefore power factor <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#981;</mi></math> will be zero.</p>

</details>

---

### Q52. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In an ac circuit, an inductor, a capacitor and a resistor are connected in series with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>.</mo></math> Impedance of this circuit is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Zero
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Recall the formula of impedance is in terms of resistance, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></math>, here, the condition of resonance, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math>, it means, 
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q53. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An inductor coil stores <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>64</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> of magnetic field energy and dissipates energy at the rate of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>640</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math> when a current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is passed through it. If this coil is joined across an ideal battery, find the time constant of the circuit (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>).

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>125</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dc3b4c86-73d2-46ba-9d37-01f2c95eb585-image.png" style="width: 288px; height: 198px;" /></p><p>Energy stored in an inductor, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>L</mi><msup><mi>I</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>64</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>L</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>
and power dissipated in a resistor, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>640</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>
Time constant of the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>D</mi><mi>C</mi></math> circuit is,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#964;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q54. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A series LCR circuit driven by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> at a frequency of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math> contains a resistance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>k&#937;</mi></math>, an inductor of inductive reactance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>250</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> and an unknown capacitor. The value of capacitance to maximise the average power should be:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mtext>take</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;F</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Angular frequency, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>At resonance,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>&#969;C</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#969;L</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>&#969;C</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>250</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>100</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>250</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;F</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q55. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> resistance, a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> capacitor and an inductor are connected in series across a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> supply at variable frequency. Calculate the value of inductance of inductor at which resonance will occur. Given that the resonant frequency is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math>.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>70</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>70</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>mH</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>03</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>70</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>70</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math></p><p>Resonant frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>LC</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msubsup><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>by putting values <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>&#8771;</mo><mn>70</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>07</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> inductor and a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> resistor are connected in series to a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>220</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math> AC source. The approximate current in the circuit and the phase angle between current and source voltage are respectively.</p><p>[Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#960;</mi></math> as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open=""><mfrac><mn>22</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>88</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>88</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>6</mn><mn>11</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mi>R</mi></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>L</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">&#160;</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>22</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>07</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>22</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>22</mn><mn>12</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msubsup><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>059</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>V</mi><mi>Z</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>220</mn><mrow><mn>25</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>059</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>77</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 2)

mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mi>rms</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><msqrt><msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">c</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">L</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>25</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>x</mi><mi ### Q58. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

An inductor of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math> is connected to a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> battery through a resistor of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>k&#937;</mi></math> and a switch. After a long time, when maximum current is set up in the circuit, the current is switched off. The current in the circuit after <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;s</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math>. Then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is equal to ______ . (Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>37</mn></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **74**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>V</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>K&#937;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math></p><p>For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></math>-decay circuit, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>I</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>37</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>74</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>74</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q59. JEE Main 2021 (22 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Match List-I with List-II.</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"> </td>			<td style="text-align: center;">List - I</td>			<td style="text-align: center;"> </td>			<td style="text-align: center;">List - II</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;">(a)</td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">(i)</td>			<td style="text-align: center;">Current is in phase with emf</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;">(b)</td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">(ii)</td>			<td style="text-align: center;">Current lags behind the applied emf</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;">(c)</td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">(iii)</td>			<td style="text-align: center;">Maximum current occurs</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;">(d)</td>			<td style="text-align: center;">Resonant frequency</td>			<td style="text-align: center;">(iv)</td>			<td style="text-align: center;">Current leads the emf</td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** (a) - (ii) ; (b) - (i) ; (c) - (iv) ; (d) - (iii)
- **(B)** (a) - (ii) ; (b) - (i) ; (c) - (iii) ; (d) - (iv)
- **(C)** (a) - (iii) ; (b) - (i) ; (c) - (iv); (d) - (ii)
- **(D)** (a) - (iv) ; (b) - (iii) ; (c) - (ii); (d) - (i)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) (a) - (ii) ; (b) - (i) ; (c) - (iv) ; (d) - (iii)**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(a) For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>,</mo></math> voltage leads the current</p><p>(ii)</p><p>(b) For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>,</mo></math> voltage <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo mathvariant="italic">&#38;</mo></math> current are in same phase</p><p>(i)</p><p>(c) For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>,</mo></math> current leads the voltage</p><p>(iv)</p><p>(d) For resonant frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>,</mo></math> current is maximum</p><p>(iii)</p>

</details>

### Q60. JEE Main 2021 (20 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A series LCR circuit of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>F</mi></math> is connected across an <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AC</mi></math> supply of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo></math> having variable frequency. The power dissipated at resonance condition is ________<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>.</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **125**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math> (due to resonance)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi></math> so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>V</mi><mi>Z</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>V</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>250</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>250</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>125</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q61. JEE Main 2021 (20 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

For a series <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>LCR</mi></math> circuit with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>pF</mi></math> connected across <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>220</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AC</mi></math> supply, the phase angle between
current and supplied voltage and the nature of the circuit is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>,</mo></math> resistive circuit
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>,</mo></math> predominantly inductive circuit
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>,</mo></math> resonance circuit
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>,</mo></math> predominantly capacitive circuit

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>,</mo></math> predominantly capacitive circuit**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>11</mn></msup><mrow><mn>100</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mo>&#8811;</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub></math></p><p>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><msub><mi>X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8811;</mo><mi>R</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q62. JEE Main 2021 (20 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

In an <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>R</mi></math> series circuit, an inductor <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math> and a resistor <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> are connected to an <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AC</mi></math> source of angular frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> The value of capacitance for which the current leads the voltage by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>x</mi></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>. Then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>tan</mi><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>tan</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>03</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>

</details>

### Q63. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The time taken for the magnetic energy to reach <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of its maximum value, when a solenoid of resistance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math>, inductance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> is connected to a battery, is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mi>l</mi><mi>n</mi><mn>5</mn></math>
- **(B)** infinite
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mi>l</mi><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mi>l</mi><mi>n</mi><mn>10</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mi>l</mi><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Magnetic energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>L</mi><msup><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>%</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>%</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>R</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> for charging</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mi>l</mi><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q64. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In a scries <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>R</mi></math> resonance circuit, if we change the resistance only, from a lower to higher value :

**Options:**

- **(A)** The bandwidth of resonance circuit will increase.
- **(B)** The resonance frequency will increase.
- **(C)** The quality factor will increase.
- **(D)** The quality factor and the resonance frequency will remain constant.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) The bandwidth of resonance circuit will increase.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The bandwidth of resonance circuit will increase.</p><p>As we know that Bandwidth <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>L</mi></math>.</p><p>Bandwidth <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>R</mi></math>.</p><p>So, bandwidth will increase.</p>

</details>

### Q67. JEE Main 2021 (26 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

In a series LCR resonant circuit, the quality factor is measured as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn></math>. If the inductance is increased by two fold and resistance is decreased by two fold, then the quality factor after this change will be _______

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **282.84**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mfrac><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mi>L</mi></msqrt><mrow><mi>R</mi><msqrt><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>L</mi></msqrt><mrow><mfenced><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><msqrt><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mfenced><mn>100</mn></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>282</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>84</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q68. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>.</mo></math> circuit contains resistance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>110</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi><mi></mi></math> and a supply of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>220</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> angular frequency. If only capacitance is removed from the circuit, current lags behind the voltage by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>. If on the other hand, the only the inductor is removed the current leads by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> with the applied voltage. The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>M</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>.</mo></math> current flowing in the circuit will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>tan</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> resonance</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>V</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>220</mn><mn>110</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q69. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

A transmitting station releases waves of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>960</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. A capacitor of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>56</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> is used in the resonant circuit. The self-inductance of coil necessary for resonance is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>. find <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>960</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>56</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>56</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>?</mo></math></p><p>Now at resonance, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math> [Resonant frequency]</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>On substituting <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>,</mo></math> we have <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>On substituting <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>,</mo></math> we have <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>Squaring both sides: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mfrac><msup><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><msup><mfenced><mn>960</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>56</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>16</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>56</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>960</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>960</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math></p>

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>The angular frequency of alternating current in a L-C-R circuit is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The components connected are shown in the figure. Find the value of inductance of the coil and capacity of condenser.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f5afaa00-7567-4dca-be66-c710a45ece32-image.png" style="width: 384px; height: 199px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>33</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>150</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>150</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>33</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Current through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> resistance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>60</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p>thus capacitor current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>I</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>40</mn><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4000</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math></p><p>Now,</p><p>current through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> resistance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>20</mn><mn>40</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p>thus current through inductor <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>I</mi><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>L</mi></math></p><p><math ### Q71. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>The current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo></math> at time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8734;</mo></math> respectively for the given circuit is :</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/96d424cf-d5b4-40fb-87f6-17984f076b7c-image.png" style="width: 220px; height: 181px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>18</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>18</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>55</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>18</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>33</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>33</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>18</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>18</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>55</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>18</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>18</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>33</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, current through inductor is zero,</p><p>hence <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>eq</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8214;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>18</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mrow><mn>18</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>18</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8734;</mo><mo>,</mo></math> inductor becomes a simple wire and now the circuit will be as shown in figure</p><p>hence <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>eq</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>&#8741;</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#8741;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>33</mn><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>;</mo></math> (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8741;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> parallel)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mrow><mn>33</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>33</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9a019e65-ed37-4366-8f83-aa8f20cf21b2-image.png" style="width: 220px; height: 164px;" /></p>

</details>

A series LCR circuit is designed to resonate at an angular frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> The circuit draws <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math> power from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>120</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> source at resonance. The value of resistance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> in the circuit is ________ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi><mo>.</mo></math>

### Q74. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A series <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R</mi></math> circuit is connected to a battery of emf <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math>. If the circuit is switched on at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, then the time at which the energy stored in the inductor reaches <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>n</mi></mfrac></mfenced></math> times of its maximum value, is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mfrac><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt><mrow><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mfrac><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt><mrow><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mfrac><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt><mrow><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/307d6688-be2b-4460-96e0-29f818ecbcd0-image.png" style="width: 104px; height: 43px;" /></p><p>Potential energy stored in inductor is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8746;</mo><mo>&#8733;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>RT</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mi>ln</mi><mfrac><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msqrt><mrow><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>  </p>

</details>

/></p><p>Here,<math <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mi xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>20</mn><mn>835</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>20</mn><mn>835</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi </details>

### Q76. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Two identical capacitors <math><mi>A</mi></math> and <math><mi>B</mi><mo>,</mo></math> charged to the same potential <math><mn>5</mn><mi>V</mi></math> are connected in two different circuits as shown below at time <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo></math> If the charge on capacitors <math><mi>A</mi></math> and <math><mi>B</mi></math> at time <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>R</mi></math> is <math><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub></math> respectively, then (Here <math><mi>e</mi></math> is the base of natural logarithm)

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/87177991-a74d-479d-8bcb-e0ca05b76539-image.png" style="width: 390px; height: 170px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Charge on capacitor after charging with a battery of potential <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></math></p><p>Now, capacitor <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is connected as shown in the figure.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d1d3ee45-5844-40b4-b82b-9bf023758c1f-image.png" style="width: 251px; height: 184px;" /></p><p>As diode is in reverse bias, so capacitor charge remains same after time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>R</mi></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></math></p><p>In case <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>, capacitor is connected as shown in the figure.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e81d64e8-39aa-4ccf-ada5-469783323c73-image.png" style="width: 249px; height: 177px;" /></p><p>Here, diode is in forward bias, so capacitor discharges and its charge decays as </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#183;</mo><msup><mi>e</mi><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac></msup></math></p><p>where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>C</mi></math></p>

</details>
