### Q77. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In <math><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></math> circuit the inductance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math> and capacitance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;F</mi><mo>.</mo></math> If a voltage <math><mi>V</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>314</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is applied to the circuit, the current in the circuit is given as:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0.52</mn><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>314</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>314</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5.2</mn><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>314</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0.52</mn><mi>sin</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>314</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0.52</mn><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>314</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img height="171" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1c90a52b-3b5f-4c9f-8de6-0469eceb372c-image.png" width="223" />
<math><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi></mi><msqrt><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></math>
<math><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow></msub></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>L</mi></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>314</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>314</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>31.84</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>12.56</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>19.28</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7f551326-b194-40b4-894f-61b4a36e935a-image.png" style="width: 156px; height: 178px;" />
<math><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>Z</mi></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>314</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow><mi></mi></math>
<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>Z</mi></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>314</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>19.28</mn></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>314</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></math>
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.52</mn><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>314</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math>

</details>

### Q78. JEE Main 2020 (08 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/39fd6419-0eb4-4bad-8590-f252b87abb14.png" />
As shown in the figure, a battery of emf <math><mo>&#8712;</mo></math> is connected to an inductor <math><mi>L</mi></math> and resistance <math><mi>R</mi></math> in series. The switch is closed at <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo></math> The total charge that flows from the battery, between <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></msub></math> ( <math><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></msub></math> is the time constant of the circuit) is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mo>∈</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mo>∈</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mo>∈</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mo>∈</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mo>∈</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><mi>q</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><munderover><mo stretchy="false">&#8747;</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></munderover><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mrow><mi></mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi></mfrac><msubsup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>t</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>c</mi></msub></mfrac></mrow></msup><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>c</mi></msub></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mn>0</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>c</mi></msub></msubsup><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>c</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8712;</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q79. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An emf of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> is applied at time <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> to a circuit containing in series <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> inductor and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>&#937;</mtext></math> resistor. The ratio of the currents at time <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#8734;</mi></math> and at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>s</mtext></math> is close to: (Take <math><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>7.389</mn></math> )

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>1.06</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>1.15</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>1.46</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>0.84</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mn>1.06</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/568f625d-f7f2-458e-aa1a-d05a04b855e8-image.png" />
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0.01</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>500</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p></blockquote><blockquote><p>now at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8734;</mo></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8734;</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p></blockquote><blockquote><p>at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mtext>&#160;s</mtext></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>40</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>40</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>10000</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>7.29</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>10000</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8734;</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>40</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>1</mn></math> Slightly greater then one.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q80. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A <math><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> circuit behaves like a clamped harmonic oscillator. Comparing it with a physical spring-mass damped oscillator having damping constant <math><mi>&#39;</mi><msup><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#39;</mi></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi></mi></math> the correct equivalence would be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>L</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi>C</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>b</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>L</mi><mo>↔</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi>C</mi><mo>↔</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>↔</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>L</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi>C</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>m</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>L</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi>C</mi><mo>↔</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>b</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mi>L</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi>C</mi><mo>↔</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>b</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

In damped oscillation <math><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e0fd5eae-303f-4d2d-a211-b3a47101cfad-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 185px;" />
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
In the circuit
<math><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mi></mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
Comparing equation (i) and (ii)
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q81. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Consider the LR circuit shown in the figure. If the switch S is closed at <math><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> then the amount of charge that passes through the battery between <math><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> is:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/527efd6a-63ca-4856-8941-456360a46842-image.png" style="width: 290px; height: 177px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>7.3</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>7.3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2.7</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2.7</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfrac><mrow><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2.7</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The current in the LR circuit at any time <math><mi>t</mi></math> is
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>I</mi></math> are the EMF, resistance, inductance and the current respectively.
<math><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Charge flown through the circuit from time 0 to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math> is 
(charge) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mo>=</mo><munderover><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac></munderover><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><munderover><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac></munderover><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><msubsup><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mn>0</mn><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac></msubsup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2.7</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q82. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A circuit connected to an ac source of emf <math><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>100</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math> with <math><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></math> in seconds, gives a phase difference of <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> between the emf <math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>e</mi></math> and current <math><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math> . Which of the following circuits will exhibit this?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> circuit with <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> circuit with <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> circuit with <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> circuit with <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> circuit with <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn></math> rad/sec </p><p>phase difference between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>e</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi></math> is given by </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>tan</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>X</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>so   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>tan</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>tan</mi><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>in case of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mi>L</mi></math> circuit  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>L</mi></math></p><p>And in case of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mi>C</mi></math> circuit  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><strong>For option </strong><math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>:</mo></math> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> and</p><p>             <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>10000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>
              <math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow></math>
<strong>For option</strong> <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>:</mo></math> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> and</p><p>                        <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p>                       <math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow></math>
<strong>For option  </strong><math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>:</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> and</p><p>                   <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>
                    <math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow></math>
<strong>For option</strong><math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>:</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> and</p><p>                <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>
               <math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>45</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow></math></p>

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A <math><mn>20</mn></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>H</mtext></math> inductor coil is connected to a <math><mn>10</mn></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math> resistance in series as shown in figure. The time at which rate of dissipation of energy (Joule&#39;s heat) across resistance is equal to the rate at which magnetic energy is stored in the inductor, is:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/36cb4009-f746-4169-87cb-d4cdb6e75f7c-image.png" style="width: 300px; height: 180px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mfrac>  <mn>1</mn>  <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math> <mn>2</mn><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mfrac>  <mn>2</mn>  <mrow>   <mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></mrow> </mfrac> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math> <mn>2</mn><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Rate of dissipation of energy in resistor <math><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>
Rate of energy stored in inductor <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8230;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
In <math><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math> circuit:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace></math>
<math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mrow></msup></math>
From equation (i),</p><p><math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mfrac></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#964;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q84. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In the figure shown, a circuit contains two identical resistors with resistance <math><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>&#937;</mi></math> and an inductance with <math><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>m</mi><mi>H</mi><mo>.</mo></math> An ideal battery of <math><mn>15</mn><mi>V</mi></math> is connected in the circuit. What will be the current through the battery long after the switch is closed?

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/civwrfuzbhvhdnpjxmjyowjujddqconbickfdhhpxrioadaznl.png" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>5.5</mn><mi> </mi><mi>A</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>6</mn><mi> </mi><mi>A</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>3</mn><mi> </mi><mi>A</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>7.5</mn><mi> </mi><mi>A</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>6</mn><mi> </mi><mi>A</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/atiwzskbfdgjvqdqzhafgagslsuoxontwttabxyxfikmwxfhtv.png" />

After a long time, when the growth of current in the circuit has stopped, the inductor will work as a conductor. Therefore the effective resistance of the circuit will be, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p>Hence, the current through the circuit will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>V</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>2.5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>A</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q85. JEE Main 2019 (11 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In the circuit shown,
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/kbMOwC2aGptvR-vCPHDwKsu6UBwKW6tfmxuZxxHuFDM.original.fullsize.png">
the switch $S_{1}$ is closed at time $t=0$ and the switch $S_{2}$ is kept open. At some later time $\left(\mathrm{t}_{0}\right)$, the switch $\mathrm{S}_{1}$ is opened and $\mathrm{S}_{2}$ is closed. the behaviour of the current I as a function of time ' $\mathrm{t}$ ' is given by:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/WUqWNjx0bipGRxfznfB2BlAKR_19TssDxkWy4ket2Fg.original.fullsize.png">
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/27kdZt6lTf6FSkLIYCyv15tlBnDDzXqnxirGdXoEiUM.original.fullsize.png">
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/v6PwSrKR9YF5OZFYP7QQ60R1iwYwoDna_wkG7ob_4rE.original.fullsize.png">
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8zK6dkfoce0avAsTOdFUZ6KRpSqiy_baFKegII0BBB0.original.fullsize.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/WUqWNjx0bipGRxfznfB2BlAKR_19TssDxkWy4ket2Fg.original.fullsize.png">**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/tpP1_Vak70Ispq1Vqbr82YwXBdCaF6VF-J6AYsVQfB0.original.fullsize.png">
The current will grow for the time $\mathrm{t}=0$ to $\mathrm{t}=\mathrm{t}_{0}$ and after that decay of current takes place.
Growth and decay of current is of exponential nature
$\mathrm{i}=\mathrm{i}_0\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{t} / \mathrm{\tau}}\right) \rightarrow \text { during growth }$
$\mathrm{i}=\mathrm{i}_{\max } \mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{t} / \mathrm{\tau}} \rightarrow \text { during decay }$

</details>

### Q86. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

An ideal capacitor of capacitance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.2</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;F</mi></math> is charged to a potential difference of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. The charging battery is then disconnected. The capacitor is then connected to an ideal inductor of self inductance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mH</mi></math>. The current at a time when the potential difference across the capacitor is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>17</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>34</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>17</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The energy stored in the capacitor is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>. The energy stored in the inductor is given by. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>L</mi><msup><mi>I</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>. In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></math> circuit there is no loss of energy in the form of heat so, using energy conservation, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mrow><mi>L</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mrow><mi>L</mi><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0.2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0.2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0.5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi>I</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.17</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q87. JEE Main 2017 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A sinusoidal voltage of peak value <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>283</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> and angular frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>320</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> is applied to a series <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>R</mi></math> circuit. Given that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5&#160;</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>mH</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;F</mi></math>.<mo></mo><mn></mn><mi></mi><mi></mi> The total impedance and phase difference between the voltage across the source and the current will respectively be-

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>10&#160;</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d&#160;</mi><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>7 </mn><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mn>45</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>7 </mn><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d </mi><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>10</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d </mi><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>7 </mn><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mn>45</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>283</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>volt</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>320&#160;</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>320</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>320</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>10000</mn><mn>320</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3.1</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>

<math><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>

<math><mi>Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><msqrt><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><msqrt><mn>50&#160;</mn></msqrt><mn>&#8776; 7</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>tan</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>X</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#10233;</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>45</mn><mi>o</mi></msup></math>

</details>

### Q88. JEE Main 2016 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A series <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></math> circuit is connected to a voltage source with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>t</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> . After a very large time, current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>t</mi></mfenced></math> behaves as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>&#8811;</mo><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d07e3ac2-1cc2-4927-8634-6c7f9364b656-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 121px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cdfd53a4-0ce8-4cbb-af67-3893e3954917-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 126px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ace8bde8-07ec-4ce7-81b3-cf019f42e959-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 128px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/548edd5b-f379-46ce-8def-8ffd479b26a2-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 135px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/548edd5b-f379-46ce-8def-8ffd479b26a2-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 135px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>When sinusoidal voltage is applied across <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></math> circuit, current is also sinusoidal but it lags by some phase.</p><p>Current will be in the form of
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>sin</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
Graph will be sinusoidal.</p><p>Here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is the maximum current and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi></math> is phase difference between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>I</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q89. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An arc lamp requires a direct current of 10 A at 80 V to function. If it is connected to a 220 V (rms), 50 Hz AC supply, the series inductor needed for it to work is close to:

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.044 H
- **(B)** 0.065 H
- **(C)** 80 H
- **(D)** 0.08 H

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 0.065 H**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/249782b5-75b0-4427-9d3e-c4f6e77fa2c1-image.png" style="width: 267px; height: 193px;" />
Given that the lamp draws a current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> upon a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>80</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> D.C. supply, the resistance is,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>80</mn><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p>When connected to an AC supply with an inductor in series, as shown in the figure above, the current,</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p>and the voltage drop over the lamp is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>80</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></math> over the inductance.</p><p>Applying the rules of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R</mi></math> circuit,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>80</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>220</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>48400</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>6400</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mi>210</mi></math> V

The Inductive reactance of the given inductor of inductance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math>, for the frequency of supply <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;fL</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>The voltage drop across the inductance,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;I</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>210</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mi>I</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>210</mn><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>21</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#937;</mi></math></p><p>Substituting values in equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>21</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>f</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>21</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>50</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>21</mn><mrow><mn>100</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0.065</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q90. JEE Main 2015 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

For the LCR circuit, shown here, the current is observed to lead the applied voltage. An additional capacitor <math><msup><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8242;</mi></mrow></msup></math> , when joined with the capacitor C present in the circuit, makes the power factor of the circuit unity. The capacitor <math><msup><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8242;</mi></mrow></msup></math> , must have been connected in:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/af3672ee-132c-434d-b279-60cad76f8420-image.png" style="width: 180px; height: 144px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** Parallel with C and has a magnitude <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** Series with C and has a magnitude  <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** Series with C and has a magnitude  <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** Parallel with C and has a magnitude  <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Parallel with C and has a magnitude <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f6eb8379-730d-42ac-9616-bfb9f18f2e1f-image.png" style="width: 130px; height: 154px;" />

Current is leading,  it means inductive reactance is more than capacitive reactance

For making power factor to be unity the value of C is to be increased.

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> another capacitor <math><msup><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8242;</mi></mrow></msup></math> should be connected in parallel. So in that condition <math><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8242;</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8242;</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>C</mi></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8242;</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q91. JEE Main 2015 (04 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An inductor <math> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.03</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>H</mtext></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow> </math> and a resistor <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>k&#937;</mtext><mo>)</mo></math> are connected in series to a battery of <math> <mn>15</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>V</mtext> </math> E.M.F. in a circuit shown below. The key <math><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math> has been kept closed for a long time. Then at <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, <math><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math> is opened and key <math><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> is closed simultaneously. At <math> <mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>ms</mtext> </math> , the current in the circuit will be : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mtext>Take</mtext><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>e</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>150</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/P_52_Ques_A.jpg" style="width: 208px; height: 167px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>67</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>67</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>67</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mA</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<strong>Case I:</strong> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">K</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is closed for long time, 

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/P_55_Ans_Correction_2.jpg" style="width: 224px; height: 136px;" />

for a long time, the inductor acts as a conducting wire, <math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;</mo> </mrow> </math>current in the circuit <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">V</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">R</mtext></mfrac></math><math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>5</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>&#8658;</mtext><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">i</mtext><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mtext>0.1 A</mtext></math>
<strong>Case II:</strong> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">K</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is open and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">K</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is closed

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/P_52_Ans_3.jpg" />

Current in the circuit, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">i</mtext><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">i</mtext><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mtext>e</mtext><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">t</mtext></mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mfrac></msup><mtext>;&#160;&#160;</mtext><mi>&#964;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">L</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">R</mtext></mfrac></math>

After <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">t</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>ms</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mn>0</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>s</mtext></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>&#8658;</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">i</mtext><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">i</mtext><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mtext>e</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><msup><mn>0</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mn>0</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></mrow></msup></math><math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>0.1</mtext> <msup> <mtext>e</mtext> <mfrac> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </msup> </mrow> </math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mtext>0.1</mtext><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mtext>e</mtext><mn>5</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mtext>0.1</mtext><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>A</mtext></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mtext>0.67&#160;</mtext><mo>mA</mo></math>.

</details>

### Q92. JEE Main 2015 (04 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

An LCR circuit is equivalent to a damped pendulum. In an LCR circuit the capacitor is charged to <math><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> and then connected to the L and R as shown below:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3b085824-196f-41fc-b86d-ebc01080c60d-image.png" style="width: 253px; height: 165px;" />
If a student plots graphs of the square of maximum charge <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mfenced></math> on the capacitor with time (t) for two different values <math><msub><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>(</mo><msub><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>)</mo></math> of L then which of the following represents this graph correctly? (plots are schematic and not drawn to scale)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/307f8cc7-a004-4867-ba7b-8b31559a5c46-image.png" style="width: 264px; height: 164px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/539281b2-eb0c-4827-9e86-e7ddb12e6734-image.png" style="width: 283px; height: 204px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/31d5482b-fcf5-49a5-a14d-d47a94b7b6b6-image.png" style="width: 261px; height: 181px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3d3836f2-3b04-4e6e-88d7-07cd6d4797a8-image.png" style="width: 266px; height: 190px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/31d5482b-fcf5-49a5-a14d-d47a94b7b6b6-image.png" style="width: 261px; height: 181px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

Comparing to the damped pendulum. We write, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">m</mtext><mfrac><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">v</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">t</mtext></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">kx</mtext><mo>-</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">bv</mtext></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>b</mi><mi>v</mi></math> is resistive force,<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;</mo> </mrow> </math> Amplitude, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">A</mtext><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">A</mtext><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mtext>e</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">bt</mtext><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext mathvariant="italic">m</mtext></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>. Comparing results, we can write

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">q</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">c</mtext></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">L</mtext><mfrac><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">i</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">t</mtext></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">iR</mtext></math>, as charge decreasing, 
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3b085824-196f-41fc-b86d-ebc01080c60d-image.png" style="width: 253px; height: 165px;" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">q</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">c</mtext></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">L</mtext><mfrac><mrow><msup><mtext>d</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mtext mathvariant="italic">q</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><msup><mtext mathvariant="italic">t</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">q</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">t</mtext></mrow></mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">R</mtext></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">A</mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">q</mtext><mtext>;&#160;</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">R</mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">b</mtext><mtext>,&#160;</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">m</mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">L</mtext></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>&#8658;</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">q</mtext><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">q</mtext><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mtext>e</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">Rt</mtext><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext mathvariant="italic">L</mtext></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>&#8658;</mtext><msup><mtext mathvariant="italic">q</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mtext mathvariant="italic">q</mtext><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><msup><mtext>e</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">Rt</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">L</mtext></mfrac></mrow></msup></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>&#8658;</mtext><msup><mtext mathvariant="italic">q</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mtext mathvariant="italic">q</mtext><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><msup><mtext>e</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">Rt</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">L</mtext></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>.

Exponentially decreasing function, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> graph has higher values than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>.

</details>

### Q93. JEE Main 2014 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

When the rms voltages <math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>V</mi> <mi>L</mi> </msub> <mo>,</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><msub> <mi>V</mi> <mi>C</mi> </msub> </mrow> </math> and <math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>V</mi> <mi>R</mi> </msub> </mrow> </math> are measured respectively across the inductor <math> <mrow> <mi>L</mi><mo>,</mo></mrow> </math> the capacitor <math> <mi>C</mi> </math> and the resistor <math> <mi>R</mi> </math> in a series <math> <mrow> <mi>L</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow> </math> circuit connected to an <math> <mrow> <mi>A</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow> </math> source, it is found that the ratio <math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>V</mi> <mi>L</mi> </msub> <mo>:</mo><msub> <mi>V</mi> <mi>C</mi> </msub> <mo>:</mo><msub> <mi>V</mi> <mi>R</mi> </msub> <mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>3.</mn></mrow> </math> If the rms voltage of the <math> <mrow> <mi>A</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow> </math> source is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8201;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo></math> then <math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>V</mi> <mi>R</mi> </msub> </mrow> </math> is close to :

### Q94. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In the circuit shown here, the point &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math>&#39; is kept connected to point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> &#39;&#39; till the current flowing through the circuit becomes constant. Afterward, suddenly, point &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math>&#39; is disconnected from point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> &#39;&#39; and connected to point &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>&#39; at time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. Ratio of the voltage across resistance and the inductor at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math> will be equal to :

          <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/EM0025741_Question.jpg" style="width: 242px; height: 130px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>e</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>e</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** 1
- **(C)** - 1
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>e</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>e</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>R</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></math>
 </p>

### Q95. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

When resonance is produced in a series LCR circuit, then which of the following is not correct?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Current in the circuit is in phase with the applied voltage.
- **(B)** Inductive and capacitive reactances are equal.
- **(C)** If $\mathrm{R}$ is reduced, the voltage across capacitor will increase.
- **(D)** Impedance of the circuit is maximum.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Impedance of the circuit is maximum.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Impedance $(Z)$ of the series LCR circuit is
$Z=\sqrt{R^2+\left(X_L-X_C\right)^2}$
At resonance, $\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{L}}=\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{C}}$
Therefore, $Z_{\text {minimum }}=R$

</details>

### Q97. JEE Main 2013 (22 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An LCR circuit as shown in the figure is connected to a voltage source $\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{ac}}$ whose frequency can be varied.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/aMUN3q9hjrgt0pbKw-d5lIVIkxwfEwHbjoBB6qCecME.original.fullsize.png">

The frequency, at which the voltage across the resistor is maximum, is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $902 \mathrm{~Hz}$
- **(B)** $143 \mathrm{~Hz}$
- **(C)** $23 \mathrm{~Hz}$
- **(D)** $345 \mathrm{~Hz}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $23 \mathrm{~Hz}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Frequency $\mathrm{f}=\frac{1}{2 \pi \sqrt{\mathrm{LC}}}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =\frac{1}{2 \times 3.4 \sqrt{24 \times 2 \times 10^{-6}}} \\
& \simeq 23 \mathrm{~Hz}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q98. JEE Main 2013 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In the circuit shown here, the voltage across $\mathrm{E}$ and $\mathrm{C}$ are respectively $300 \mathrm{~V}$ and $400 \mathrm{~V}$. The voltage $\mathrm{E}$ of the ac source is :
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/YSP4MM9BEDO78fWILiVDleUky9_Ezn_8B4xtE3kK1gA.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $400$ Volt
- **(B)** $500$ Volt
- **(C)** $100$ Volt
- **(D)** $700$ Volt

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $100$ Volt**

**Detailed Solution:**

Voltage $E$ of the ac source
$\begin{aligned}
& E=V_C-V_L \\
& =400 \mathrm{~V}-300 \mathrm{~V}=100 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q99. JEE Main 2013 (07 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The amplitude of a damped oscillator decreases to 0.9 times its original magnitude in 5s. In another 10s it will decrease to <math> <mrow> <mi>&#945;</mi> </mrow> </math> times its original magnitude, where <math> <mrow> <mi>&#945;</mi> </mrow> </math> equals :

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.729
- **(B)** 0.6
- **(C)** 0.7
- **(D)** 0.81

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 0.729**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>A</mtext><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext>A</mtext><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mtext>&#160;e</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mtext>bt</mtext><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext>m</mtext></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>

After 5 second

<math> <mrow> <mtext>0.9</mtext> <msub> <mtext>A</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>A</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msup> <mtext>&#160;e</mtext> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>b</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>   ...... (i)

After 10 more second

<math> <mrow> <mtext>A</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>A</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msup> <mtext>&#160;e</mtext> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>b</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>     ...... (ii)

From (i) and (ii)

<math> <mrow> <mtext>A</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>0.729 A</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3 R C \ell n 2$
- **(B)** $2 R C \ell n 2$
- **(C)** $2 R C \ln 7$
- **(D)** $3 R C \ell n 7$

**Detailed Solution:**
